Wait, if Black plays c6d4, it's Nc6xd4. White can then recapture with a piece or a pawn? Let's check. White's pawns are on a2, b2, c2, f2, g2, h2. d2 and e2 are empty. So if Black plays Nc6xd4, White cannot recapture with a pawn. However, White has knights on d4 (which is being captured) and f3. White might recapture with the knight on f3 (Nf3xd4).

Wait, if Black plays Nc6xd4 and then White plays Nf3xd4, White has lost a centrally placed knight and Black has successfully traded.

Other options:
c6e5: Controls the center, attacks d3 (which is empty).
f7f6: Prepares to challenge the center or develop.
e6e5: Controls center.

Usually, in such positions, trading the active central knight of the opponent is a good idea.
Let's verify if c6d4 is indeed a capture.
Board layout:
Row 4: . . . N (d4) . . . .
Row 6: . . N (c6) . . . . . .
Move c6d4 is a knight move from c6 to d4. Since d4 is occupied by a white knight, it is a capture (Nc6xd4).

Is there a better move?
If Black doesn't trade, the knight on d4 remains very powerful.
Developing the bishop from f8 or d8 (queen) are also options, but Nc6xd4 seems strong as it removes White's most active piece.

Let's consider c6e5. This maintains the knight and controls d3 and f3.
Let's consider the move e6e5. This gains space in the center.

However, Nc6xd4 looks like the most direct way to reduce White's influence.

Let's double check the legal moves list again:
c6d4 is there.
c6e5 is there.
e6e5 is there.

Looking at the white side: Nf3 and Nd4 are well-placed.
Black has pawns on a6, b7, e6, f7, g7, h7.
White's pawns on c2 and f2 suggest they are avoiding the center for now.

If I play c6d4, then White's most likely response is Nf3xd4.
Then Black has essentially traded a knight for a knight, removing the strong d4 piece.

Is there any tactic I'm missing?
White's knight on f3 is not defended by a pawn (e2 is empty).
But White's knight on d4 is also not defended by a pawn (c3, e3 are empty).
So Nc6xd4 is a straight trade.

Comparing c6d4 vs e6e5:
e6e5 claims center space and prevents d4 knight from moving to e6 or f5 easily (though it's already at d4).
c6d4 removes the knight.

I'll go with c6d4 to eliminate the centralized knight.
